# llp-orient

<!-- @ref https://github.com/ccheever/llp/blob/v0.5.2/llp/0008-distributed-agent-skills.rfc.md#the-skills--core-five-plus-flags — skill contract -->
<!-- @ref https://github.com/ccheever/llp/blob/v0.5.2/llp/0000-linked-literate-programming.explainer.md#2-reference-syntax — the grammar this reads -->

Load the design rationale governing the area you're about to touch, before touching it. This is the consumption side of LLP — orienting first is what turns `@ref` from a possibility into a habit.

Invoke as `/llp-orient <path-or-task>` (file, directory, subsystem, or task description). With no argument, use the current change scope (`git diff --name-only`).

## Trigger

Start of a coding task that touches an area with documented design. The orient-first *rule* lives in the repo's always-loaded `AGENTS.md`; this skill is the detailed form that rule routes to for non-trivial work — don't rely on ambient description-match alone.

## Invariants

- MUST be read-only: never edit code or LLP documents.
- MUST scope to the task: pull the constraints that bind this change, not a corpus summary.
- MUST cite, not restate: reference policy as `LLP NNNN#anchor` rather than paraphrasing sections.
- MUST NOT invent constraints. If something isn't documented, say so.
- MUST NOT guess the rationale behind a broken `@ref` — report it and hand off to `llp-maintain`.

## Workflow

> **Recipe (advisory)** — corpus root = nearest `llp/` walking up from the path in scope (LLP 0001 §Unit of adoption); "no corpus" is an empty walk, not cwd-relative absence. Read `llp/foundation/` first (the kernel; the root LLP is there once `Active` — on a fresh adoption it is still `Draft` and sits in `current/`), then skim `llp/current/` (the declared working set); both hold relative links — symlinks, or small files containing the relative path — so `ls` answers and either form resolves. Then the root LLP's systems vocabulary. `Grep '@ref'` over the files in scope and `Read` each target — these are the highest-signal context. `Grep` the `**Systems:**` headers across `llp/` for LLPs governing the subsystems touched; read the governing ones (Spec, Decision, Principles, relevant RFCs). Follow `Related:` and nearby `@ref` targets one hop out; stop there unless something is clearly load-bearing. Stale-pin nudge (skip offline): read the installed tag from the skills receipt (or a `source:` pin), compare `git ls-remote --tags <origin>` — if upstream is newer, say so in one line ("skills at v0.3.0, upstream v0.5.2 — run /llp-adopt update") and move on; never update from here.

## Artifact

A context pack with these sections, faster to read than the documents it points at:

- **Scope** — files / subsystems queried
- **Governing LLPs** — numbered list, one-line gloss each; mark which are in `foundation/` and which are in `current/` (in-flight — its guidance may still move)
- **Active constraints** — bullets, each citing `LLP NNNN#anchor`
- **Relevant rationale** — the *why*, same citation form
- **Open questions / risks** for this area
- **Likely doc updates** if the change lands
- **Hand-off** — one of `none | llp-adopt | llp-create | llp-review | llp-maintain`

## Hand-offs

- No corpus (no `llp/` walking up from the path in scope) → `llp-adopt`.
- The task needs a decision no LLP covers → `llp-create` (or `llp-review` if proposal-shaped).
- An `@ref` target or anchor doesn't resolve → `llp-maintain`.

# Snapback 4 — agent command sheet

Read this, then [LLP 3000](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (the root and
the bakeoff protocol), [LLP 2000.000](../llp/2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md)
(the design brief), [`NOT-DOING.md`](./NOT-DOING.md), and the tail of
[`JOURNAL.md`](./JOURNAL.md). `rules/RULES.md` at the repository root is
adopted here as written; LLPs for Snapback 4 are numbered **3000 and up**
(`llp-create --under 3000` for a child; extend LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000
before minting a new number).

```
cargo build                          # the binary; no JS engine, no bootstrap; crates.io plus one vendored patch (vendor/tiny_http)
scripts/test.sh                      # everything blocking (60 s budget); reports every failure, never fail-fast
./target/debug/snapback4 --help      # init [--online|--expo] · check [--cost|--residency|--fix|--json] · dev · deploy · rollback · serve · run (an op, or a job as the system) · test · why · guide · data · sql · token (a service's seat) · doctor · device · types · --version
./target/debug/snapback4 guide       # lists cards (`guide aliases` lists aliases); `guide language` for the language card — every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite (crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs)
cd apps/chat && ../../target/debug/snapback4 check     # compiles snapback/*.q, writes snapback/generated/api.ts
cd apps/chat && ../../target/debug/snapback4 dev       # serves 127.0.0.1:4400; re-adopts the schema on save
cd apps/chat && bun run dev                            # the app on the real client (?as=bob picks a persona)
bun run --cwd packages/snapback4 typecheck   # the contract, the client and the hooks typecheck
cargo test -p snapback4-core --test corpus -- --nocapture   # the conformance corpus, with skip reasons
cargo test -p snapback4-difftest     # fixed seeds, oracle regressions and replay (opt-in, no database by default)
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- compile case.q   # a generated or hand-written source's binder diagnostics ("ok" when none)
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 6 --out /tmp/quarry-difftest
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/rewrite-identity--why--NOT_FOUND
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run-devices --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 8 --out /tmp/quarry-devices   # server against both device stores; no database
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/device-sqlite--write-predicted-refused--admitted
(cd lean && lake build) && cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run-lean --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 8 --out /tmp/quarry-lean   # the engine against the Lean model (LLP 3000.035 §5.2); ~900 cases/s on 8 workers
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay 'crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/lean--kind--committed~NOT_FOUND'   # needs the model built, or SNAPBACK4_LEAN_ORACLE=<quarry-oracle>
./target/debug/snapback4 check --emit-lean schema.q   # one checked file as the Lean model's case JSON; programs outside its fragment listed with a reason
# Store tests require SNAPBACK4_EVAL_POSTGRES pointing to a private evaluation cluster.
cargo test -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres  # fails if the cluster is absent
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres -- run-stores --seed 0 --cases 1000000 --workers 8 --seconds 10800 --out /tmp/quarry-stores
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres -- replay /path/to/found-case
python3 scripts/test-difftest-scheduled.py  # runner/ticket tests; no schedule or push
scripts/difftest-scheduled.sh --checkout "$HOME/quarry-difftest-runner" --state-dir "$HOME/.snapback-quarry-difftest" --hours 3
# Operator only: install daily launchd (Darwin) or user systemd timer (Linux).
scripts/install-difftest-schedule.sh --checkout "$HOME/quarry-difftest-runner"
cargo test -p snapback4-sim          # the chat world under faults: the seven invariants
cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test exhaustive   # every sequence of two writes (two viewers, two groups): disclosure and write authority against a model of the rules
SNAPBACK4_EXHAUSTIVE_DEPTH=3 cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test exhaustive -- --ignored   # three writes: ~7,000 states, minutes
scripts/kani.sh [harness...]         # Kani proofs of the key encoding (crates/snapback4-core/src/key_proofs.rs; minutes, ~12 GB peak); outside test.sh
(cd lean && ./check.sh)              # the Lean model of Quarry's core (LLP 3000.035 §5.2): lake build (~15 s cold, needs elan), no-sorry/axiom audit, 53 examples, the quarry-oracle I/O fixture; outside test.sh; open points in lean/OPEN.md
cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test native   # native jobs: builds native-fixtures/rescore for wasm32-unknown-unknown (cold ~20 s, then cached under target/native-fixture) and runs it
bash scripts/build-wasm.sh           # the device as wasm into packages/snapback4/src/wasm/ (the web tier and the Chromium witness need it); fails when the gzip exceeds metrics/wasm-budget.json — a landing that needs the bytes raises the budget in the same commit, with the reason
node witness/idb.mjs                 # the Chromium witness: the client on the wasm device over IndexedDB (needs Chrome)
WITNESS_UDID=<dedicated-udid> node witness/expo.mjs   # published witness verb/helper; freshly install apps/chat-expo Release first (simctl uninstall a previous build: its store belongs to an earlier backend); port 4400, no Metro
node witness/scale.mjs [G] [N] [M]   # cold sync at scale: G groups × N messages × M members into the wasm device; wall time + JS heap → metrics/witness.jsonl
node scripts/measure-storage.mjs     # what the store weighs on the scale seed (rows, indexes, partition log) → metrics/storage.jsonl
                                     # SNAPBACK4_BIN=target/release/snapback4 measures the release build (throughput numbers)
node scripts/measure-client.mjs      # each client tier's bytes on the pinned toolchain → metrics/client.jsonl
node scripts/attribute-wasm.mjs [--families] [--check-families] [--check-absent] # class bytes and concrete serde families; --check-families refuses non-text readers/non-Vec writers outside the exact dynamic-envelope, counting-writer and Scan map-replay allowlists; content-buffer families name the duplicated types and refuse Deserialize representations using untagged, tag or flatten; --check-absent refuses the core's prefetch, admission or log-event symbols in the web artifact (needs twiggy; slow, outside test.sh)
bash scripts/check-release-guard.sh # the web artifact's configuration plus the debug-only `web-device-check` feature must refuse to build (in test.sh)
bash scripts/measure-wasm.sh         # the device core as wasm (crates/snapback4-device), family-checked, bound and measured → metrics/wasm.jsonl
bash scripts/build-xcframework.sh    # the device as an iOS xcframework for packages/snapback4-expo (staticlib alone, stripped; 13.2 MB with native reads)
node loopbar/run.mjs --n 3           # the loop bar: fresh agents on the published pin build reduced Clique M1–M3 → metrics/loopbar.jsonl
node scripts/publish.mjs --prepare-client # rebuild wasm + clean client, pack once for cross-host qualification
# Linux qualification host: strace >=6.8 on PATH (audits every successful TCP listen).
node scripts/qualify-linux.mjs --client /path/client.tgz --expo /path/expo.tgz --out /path/qualification # Linux suite + Ubuntu/Alpine proof, no publish
# Add --consumer-only for packed frontend/toolchain proofs without test.sh; this writes consumer.json, never a publication receipt.
node scripts/publish.mjs             # Mac: qualify/sign Darwin; optional --linux-receipt + --linux-tarball import; platforms before client
```

Crates: `snapback4-core` (value model, schema with `sync`, IR, key encoding,
SQLite store, rules with hoisting, the meter, the interpreter, the partition
log, at-most-once, the day-two classifier, wire forms), `snapback4-lang`
(Quarry lexer, parser, binder, diagnostics, the TypeScript emitter),
`snapback4` (verbs and the HTTP wire), `snapback4-sim` (the deterministic
world hosting the engine and a replica device), `snapback4-difftest` (seeded
Quarry source generator, rewrite and meter oracles, optional Postgres store
oracles with prefetch on/off, the server-against-device oracle on both device stores, the engine-against-Lean-model oracle, structural shrinking and replay; not published or linked by shipped crates), `snapback4-device` (the
interpreter over a `DeviceStore`: the core's SQLite under the native
module for Expo and Exact2 hosts, memory under wasm on the web; one
dispatcher, one suite on both)). The guide (`snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs`) is
the language card and the keyword cards; packages: `snapback4` (the client
over the device and the CLI shim), `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and `snapback4-linux-x64` (the binaries), `snapback4-expo`
(the Swift module over the device's C ABI).

What exists, by week (LLP 3000 §5):

| Week | Lands |
|---|---|
| 1 | the contract (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`), the mocked chat app against it, the hub fixture wrapper, the five floor measurements in the journal, the simulator skeleton (`crates/snapback4-sim`), the Snapback 2 corpus copied in, 0.0.1 published |
| 2 | server: SQLite transaction and interpreter, rules and uniformity, the meter, the partition log (`sync`), generations, personas, the wire, `check`/`dev`/`run`/`test`/`why`/`guide`, the seven simulator invariants |
| 3 | web client: replica store, outbox with client-minted ids, local interpreter, reactive local queries, the online-only tier, the chat app on the real client |
| 4 | Expo client, day-two scenarios, deploy, the loop bar, the Clique hub run, the comparison record |

Gotchas you cannot derive from the code:

- Own Cargo and Bun workspaces; the repository root excludes them. Run cargo/bun from `snapback4/`. Node 24.x (`engines`): run the suite with the pinned runtime on PATH, not the ambient Homebrew node.
- Web builds require `bun install --frozen-lockfile` first. Binaryen 132.0.0 is
  an exact workspace devDependency; build/measurement invoke its portable
  `node_modules/binaryen/bin/wasm-opt` through Node, never a PATH optimizer.
  Missing/mismatched dependencies and optimizer failures refuse the build.
  See README's web-device build instructions and HV round 4 for host parity.
- The suite schedules client files in one pool: `max(1, min(24,
  floor(os.availableParallelism() / 3)))` slots (3 on 10 CPUs, 24 on 96).
  This counts Linux CPU affinity and leaves room for each file's servers,
  browser renderers and workers. Acquisition and server-e2e share that pool;
  there are no extra shard processes outside it. Real-timer files listed in
  `TIMER_CLIENT_FILES` drain that pool and run individually afterward with
  unchanged timing bounds. The quote-jump browser witness remains pooled.
  The two full-count model
  files retain a separate serial slot. `SNAPBACK4_TEST_SERIAL_CLIENT_FILES=1`
  still forces one client file at a time. The external file watchdog measures
  time since a subtest started or finished: max(900 seconds, largest single
  declared guard + max(60 seconds, 25%)). There is no absolute duration cap.
  Console output does not count as progress. Serial mode uses the same bounds.
  See `scripts/run-client-tests.mjs` and the QF round-2 guard census; update
  named work counts and recheck the census when declarations change. Every
  file runs as native `node --test` with default isolation, in a process group
  whose first member is a tiny Node keeper. The runner closes the keeper's stdin
  on completion, stall or abort; only the keeper calls `kill(0, SIGKILL)`.
  Runner death also closes that pipe. An unexpectedly exited keeper fails the
  file and never authorizes a runner signal. Cleanup requires two consecutive
  completed inspections (50 ms apart) with no living group member. Observed leftovers
  fail as TEST_FILE_LEAK. Failed listings and unclassifiable PID/PPID/PGID
  prefixes remain fail-closed. A ps timeout at two seconds retries once with a
  60-second bound; all other command errors refuse immediately. Listings use
  asynchronous spawn so other files' timers and keeper EOF delivery keep running.
  File watchdogs and cleanup's five-second window use per-file monotonic time.
  Expiry requires another completed snapshot: a live group after the deadline
  refuses; two consecutive empty snapshots pass even when the second is late.
  Precisely
  recognized exit-time routing anomalies
  discard the whole snapshot and retry at most twice, 50 ms apart; persistent
  anomalies refuse and never confer ownership (LLP 3000 §7, QF2).
  Metadata is validated for group members and
  their visible descendants; unrelated rows only increment diagnostics.
  Signed Darwin UIDs, variable lstart spacing and comm spaces are accepted.
  Vanished members count as exited. Processes leaving the group (setsid,
  setpgid or detached spawns) are outside cleanup custody; visible descendants
  receive metadata validation but are never signalled by the runner. Native Node's exit
  status alone decides test success; reporter counts are diagnostic only.
  The reporter observes progress as Node delivers it: synchronous work that
  does not yield may buffer events in Node's child until that work finishes.
  Such an unreported interval is subject to the same stall bound. See QF Round 7.
  The M5 MacBook is the publication host; the office M1 is calibration-only.
- Client tests and their helpers must gate Linux APIs (`/proc`, `strace`, `ss`,
  `taskset`, namespace/network tools) at the test or call, or implement a
  portable fallback. Keep portable assertions running on Darwin and give each
  skipped witness an explicit reason. The client pool runs
  `test-platform-audit.test.mjs`: a cheap static census follows static named/namespace `.mjs`
  imports and named helpers and checks scoped Linux skips/branches/early returns.
  A gate in another test does not count. This conservative common-case guard
  does not prove dynamic imports, generated scripts or arbitrary control flow;
  review those paths and run affected tests on the publication Mac.
  Listener snapshots and syscall tracing are Linux qualification capabilities,
  never dependencies of the published `snapback4/proof` browser helper.
- Before a publication dry run, warm the test artifacts **in that publication
  worktree**, while the host is free of calibration work:
  `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS=-USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT cargo test --workspace --no-run`.
  Use the dry run's toolchain, target directory, profile/features, `RUSTFLAGS`
  and Cargo profile environment. Do not add `--release` or `--profile production`:
  the suite uses the default test profile. The suite still asks Cargo for the
  complete test inventory, runs every binary and runs doctests separately.
  Warming compiles without executing tests and supplies no qualification receipt.
- One owner on the core (LLP 3000 decision 4): the engine, the store and the client contract are written by the lead session; Codex lanes take the fixture generator, the reference apps, the device witnesses and the periphery. Do not reshape `contract.ts` from a lane; propose the change in the journal.
- No ceiling is raised in slice one (LLP 2000.000 §12). A thing that does not fit changes the design or waits.
- The journal is append-only and dated; never rewrite an entry. LLP 3000 §7 carries decisions that must outlive it.
- Nothing here depends on `snapback2/` at runtime. Copy source deliberately, split under 1,500 lines on the way in, and say so in the journal.
- Named lane: `~/projects/snapback-sb4` on `sb4/*` branches. Never `git stash`; commit checkpoints.
- The conformance corpus (`corpus/`) is Snapback 2's serialized IR and value
  model; the core keeps those serializations so it binds. A case the slice
  cannot run is skipped with a named reason, never counted as a pass.
- `snapback/generated/api.ts` is built by `check` and `dev`, never committed;
  the app's typecheck needs a `check` first (the suite does one).
- Point rule probes and `exists` require a unique key. A fully pinned superset
  of a required unique key is unique too (for example an explicit role index
  containing the unique conversation/principal pair). Nullable unique key
  arguments need non-null proof. Other existence checks use bounded populations
  in code. Named equalities covering a total unique key check extra columns on
  that one row without an additional index.
- The packaged CLI uses `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS=-USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT` during both publication tests and its production build (LLP 3000 §7). A plain production Cargo build retains the dependency's ordinary SQLite policy; use the same build input when comparing with the package. The variable is unnecessary at runtime.
- When creating a stage, `scripts/publish.mjs` runs the suite and refuses on red, builds the production binary, packs `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and verifies the installed digest, then publishes both packages. Bump `version` in `packages/snapback4/package.json` (and its `optionalDependencies`), `packages/snapback4-darwin-arm64/package.json`,
  `packages/snapback4-linux-x64/package.json` and `Cargo.toml` together; a test refuses a mismatch.
- Publication retains exact qualified tarballs under `target/publication/`. If a
  stage exists, use `node scripts/publish.mjs --resume` to validate its recorded
  suite qualification, requalify those exact bytes through the installed-consumer
  proof, and finish missing registry artifacts; an existing version must match
  its staged integrity. This also applies after `--dry-run`. An ordinary invocation
  refuses an existing stage so changed source cannot silently publish old bytes.
  Bump the version to build a new pair; preserve a partially published stage.
- Cross-host publication (LLP 3000.021 §3.3): prepare the common client once
  from clean release source, qualify that tarball on Linux, and import the
  Linux tarball plus its receipt on the Mac. Source commit, client SRI and
  Linux SRI must agree. Linux prefers working Docker/Podman, otherwise uses checksum-pinned proot
  with clean Ubuntu 24.04/Alpine root filesystems in a user cache, and refuses
  `NPM_TOKEN` or `NODE_AUTH_TOKEN`, even when empty. It never signs or packs
  the client. The Mac never rebuilds or executes Linux; `--resume` rechecks
  its copied receipt. No receipt packs a Darwin-only client; an existing
  two-platform preparation is refused on that path. Packed dependencies must
  match the staged set. Uploads use verified private snapshots, with each
  digest checked again immediately before publish.
  Commands and current qualification limits:
  [`reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md`](./reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md).
- The guide is tested text. Every ```quarry block in `crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs` and in `packages/snapback4/README.md` is compiled by `crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs` with no diagnostic and no note (the language card is also run against the engine); every refusal code the sources emit must be in `guide::CODES`; every `guide("..")` a diagnostic carries must name a card. Add a form to the language card when you add it to the parser, fence README Quarry as ```quarry, and never fence Quarry as plain text to dodge the test.
- Native jobs (LLP 3000.005) need `rustup target add wasm32-unknown-unknown`; the suite's fixture build fails loudly without it. The guest SDK `crates/snapback4-native` is a path dependency until it is published; an app's `snapback/native/Cargo.toml` names it by path.

Quarry store testing (LLP 3000.035 §5.1): `SNAPBACK4_EVAL_POSTGRES` is an
explicit evaluation connection. The factory owns one physical schema per worker,
restores its rows and bookkeeping, installs the compiled fixture, then opens the
ordinary engine. Mutation attempts restore a seeded snapshot; shrinking reuses
that same factory. `Database::connect_fresh()` is an opt-in test mode. Fresh and
restored outcomes must serialize to identical bytes. The local wire observer
retains SQLSTATE, message, detail and SQL statement before the public query API
converts a driver error to a refusal; it never changes the traffic.

Reads sweep every public boundary. Mutations compare every discovered position
twice on SQLite, and compare up to eight seeded positions per dimension on
Postgres (including first/last), plus the case's original budget. Sampling
counters are in `store_stats`; each selected position runs prefetch on and off.
Physical prefetch counters are separate from the exact public outcome record.
The widened generator (`src/widen.rs`) emits prefetch-reachable reads: pages
projecting `tags[.tag]` and `tags[name = .label]` (a `Get` and a unique
`Lookup` whose keys vary across rows) and a loop reading `tags[row.tag]`, so
`store_stats` reports marked reads, transferred rows, cases with a transfer
and `prefetch_consumption_witness_probes` (fewer database calls with prefetch
on: cached answers were consumed). A planted cached-hit-to-miss fault is caught
by the random corpus (LLP 3000.035 §5.1; the closed
[coverage ticket](../issues/closed/20261007-quarry-store-oracle-prefetch-coverage-gap.md)).
Heap is charged identically on every store and the device (LLP 3000.007
§20.24.5); any disagreement is a ticket candidate, never normalized into a pass.
The cluster regression asserts it finds no signature and refuses any new one.

`run-stores --seconds N` stops each worker between cases after N seconds; a case
and any shrinking already underway finish. `cases` remains the requested count,
`generated` is actual completed generation, and `time_limited_workers` reports
the early stop. Slowest-case timing includes generation, comparisons, shrinking
and artifact saving. It does not silently claim the requested million. The scheduled
runner rejects incomplete reports; its own `--hours` option instead chooses a
case count from a 256-case calibration of both campaigns, with 20% headroom and
a million-case cap. This is a sizing estimate, not a hard deadline.

Generator widening (`src/widen.rs`, LLP 3000.035 §5.1): from a stream
independent of the base generator (`generate::base`; a seed it leaves alone is
the base case byte for byte), cases gain prefetch-reachable reads, mutation
blocks (lets, `require`, `if`, several writes to `key` and `k2`, reads after
writes), a prelude of mutations `prep0..` run in order on the same store before
`run` (`Case::steps`, sometimes as the other viewer; `observe` then reads both
keys), and expiry (`expire at .until`, a clock advancing from `seeded_at`
through each step to `clock`, sweeps, `until: now + ttl`). Every oracle runs the
prelude (`execute::world`) and compares its outcomes (`prelude-*` fields;
`store-postgres-prelude`); a device acquires at the seed clock and catches up by
one sync round after the server's prelude. The prelude is emitted first so no
rewrite moves its sites. `lean::generate` narrows half the cases outside the
model (projections, expiry) back into its fragment; the rest stay named
unsupported (`shape`, `loop`, `table-expiry`). The report's `coverage` counts
each new feature (`block/*`, `prelude/*`, `projection/*`, `expire/*`, `clock/*`).

Quarry device testing (`src/devices.rs`, `src/device_writes.rs`, LLP 3000.035
§5.1 oracle 4): each case runs on a server engine and on a device of each store
(native SQLite at a file path, and memory), both brought up through the client's
own path: the `/schema` backend as JSON text, then `local.ts::syncRound` (capture
`sync_state`, `/sync` snapshot pages built as `serve_sync.rs` builds them,
`observe_store`, `apply` through `call_text`, catch-up stream). Never seed a
device store directly. Reads the device publishes without the server (`complete`
with `unknown` false, or a known refusal) compare the whole outcome record
except `seq` (and server-only `delivery`); every other read counts under a named
reason (`online-only-*`). Low ceilings reach the device only through `call`'s
`spent` ledger (`default - n`), compared at the server's boundary positions.
Writes go through `admit`: a device refusal must equal the server's, a
predicted row must equal the committed one (less its `pending` marker), and
after `settle` plus a sync round the device must answer `observe` as the server
does. `run-devices` accepts `--seconds`. Known findings are retained under
`cases/found/device-*` with a `DIAGNOSIS.md`; `tests/devices.rs` replays them
and `tests/smoke.rs` pins the fixed-seed signature set.

The Lean oracle (`run-lean`, LLP 3000.035 §5.2): `snapback4_lang::emit::lean`
translates each generated program's surface tree (never the binder's IR) to
the model's case JSON; `quarry-oracle` (`lean/Oracle.lean`, one process per
worker) answers one tagged outcome per line; the engine runs on SQLite at
default ceilings. Compared: kind, refusal code, data (tagged: an id is not a
text), `complete`, `next` presence, each side's own continuation, and a
mutation's `observe` reread. Not compared: meter fields and sites. Programs
outside the model's fragment are named unsupported reasons, never passes.
`lean::generate` adds Lean-only variants (per-effect rules, a hidden probe
target, nullable/id orders, residual filters) aimed at `lean/OPEN.md`. A
disagreement is a Rust defect, a model defect (only when a document
contradicts the model: fix the model, rerun `lean/check.sh`), or an open
point (record the engine's behaviour in `OPEN.md`); never change the model to
match the engine without a document. Findings live in
`cases/found/lean--*` with `DIAGNOSIS.md`; `tests/lean.rs` replays them and
pins the open signature set. Lean-dependent tests skip loudly without the
built model; `SNAPBACK4_REQUIRE_LEAN=1` makes that a failure.

Quarry scheduled testing: prepare a separate clean clone whose current branch
can fast-forward to `origin/main`. The runner fetches/fast-forwards it and starts
its own socket-only evaluation cluster with `fsync=off`,
`synchronous_commit=off`, `full_page_writes=off`, `shared_buffers=1GB`, and
`max_connections=200`. It builds the Lean model's `quarry-oracle` first
(`lake build` in `lean/`; a host without `lake` fails the run), then runs the
SQLite, store, device and Lean campaigns on the selected
seed window, replays shrunk findings twice, and files/appends open `issues/*.md`
tickets. Only ticket changes are committed/pushed, with three fetch/rebase
retries. State and reports live outside Git under `--state-dir`; seeds advance
only after completed work and successful owned-cluster cleanup. Dirty/diverged
trees, overlap, setup failures, partial reports and failed publication refuse.
A crash can retain the lock and cluster directory; inspect `lock/owner.json`
and the run report. Never stop a cluster by process name.

`--dry-run --cases 32` writes previews and a separate `next-seed.dry-run.json`,
never commits or pushes. Dry runs may explicitly supply `--external-postgres
'host=... user=... dbname=...'`; that cluster is neither started nor stopped by
the runner. Ambient connections do not override normal private-cluster ownership.
The installer remains an operator action; do not invoke it during development.

The Lean oracle on a scheduled host (one-time, per user, no sudo): install
elan with `curl https://raw.githubusercontent.com/leanprover/elan/master/elan-init.sh -sSf | sh -s -- -y --default-toolchain none`.
The runner finds `lake` on `PATH` or in `~/.elan/bin`; the first `lake build`
downloads the toolchain pinned in `lean/lean-toolchain` (Lean 4.34.1, no
Mathlib), so the first scheduled run needs network. Check by hand with
`(cd lean && ./check.sh)` in the runner's clone. `run.json` records the pinned
toolchain and the oracle binary's digest.

 ref. No base-diff review is claimed for Muse. Earlier Muse attempts returned no

 Readiness records the review outcome, not author acceptance of this RFC.
+
+## 10. Development reset diagnosis (KIT4, 2026-10-18)
+
+Implementer: Codex. After a successful /dev/reset the host emits
+X-Snapback-Dev-Reset with its replacement store identity on timed JSON replies
+and exposes it through CORS. It is absent on serve and before the first reset;
+no retired session tokens or unbounded identity history are retained.
+An online client which already bound a different store halts with E_DEV_RESET,
+message "reopen the client after /dev/reset". A new client binds normally.
+This refines the existing E_STORE_REPLACED terminal state: all queued intent,
+upload, delivery and receipt unknown-outcome fences apply unchanged, with no
+automatic retry. createApp retains its existing controlled reopen path. Local
+replicas retain their existing store reacquisition contract and may internally
+normalize reset evidence to the existing E_STORE_REPLACED fence while reacquiring. Generic replacement
+and restored-sequence refusals keep their existing codes. Authentication and
+store identity checks remain authoritative; this header confers no authority.

+
+## 8. Wave 15 array alias paths (KIT4)
+
+Implementer: Codex, 2026-10-18. Dotted fixture paths traverse arrays with a
+canonical zero-based decimal segment: $alias.0.id selects the first saved
+query row's id. Object segments remain exact keys, including numeric keys.
+Negative, nonnumeric, noncanonical (00) and out-of-range array indexes refuse
+E_FIXTURE_ALIAS naming the full path and failing segment. No wire, ID minting,
+query order or assertion matching semantics change.

 **LANG3 wave-14 amendment:** [LLP 3000.036.002 §12](./3000.036.002-intent-aware-lints-and-bootstrap.rfc.md#12-wave-14-lang3-surviving-audiences-and-diagnostic-consequence) governs finite-enum Boolean/surviving-path narrowing, sorted complete-population provenance, and proof-aware immutable/required-lookup diagnostics. +### 20.12.2 Wave 15 clock evidence (KIT4, 2026-10-18)
+
+Implementer: Codex. A coalesced replay command includes --port with the actual
+registered development listener, including memory hosts. An each-boundary
+reason names every fired window. offsetMs and clockOffsetMs report the exact
+requested process offset (initial offset plus accepted advances; reset clears
+it); skewMs remains the observed authoritative now minus sampled wall time.
+Thus ordinary elapsed milliseconds and refused advances cannot change the
+reported configured offset. The durable disclosure floor still governs now.
+Neither job authority, production scheduling nor expiry semantics change.

 ## 1. Contract
@@ -644,3 +644,40 @@ The [identical blind brief](./reviews/3000.011.003.000.000-wave14-kit3-r8.brief.
 and all earlier received rounds are retained with provenance. Later commits
 `e0af692817` and `74ee49d4df` correct the Expo thread's proof scope, configured browser selection
 in the test harness, and guide/census assertions and custom-Home qualification scope; they add no semantic contract.
+
+## 12. Wave 15 proof diagnostics and clock sampling (KIT4)
+
+Implementer: Codex, 2026-10-18, commissioned for 0.4.32. On a canonical
+argument mismatch the shared E_WITNESS_UNCLAIMED message names the differing
+top-level keys. withBrowser installs a diagnostic binding which writes the
+full armed and actual arguments to a fresh mode-0600 *.witness.private.jsonl
+file under the proof log directory. Receipt messages contain no argument values.
+Diagnostic failure never cancels, retries or blocks the application's write.
+This supersedes §11's suppression of argument values in all retained logs;
+private proof logs intentionally contain sensitive application evidence.
+
+Substring timeout advice requires an explicitly scoped renderedText or
+waitForFresh locator and a substring within that scope. Whole-page prose
+cannot trigger the hint. A timeout includes up to three visible page headings
+(300 characters total); it does not weaken exact matching or ambiguity errors.
+
+waitForClock(page, advancedNow, {timeoutMs=30000}) polls the registered
+witness's signed-in client.now(), returning its number once it reaches the
+advance reply's now. It never sets page/driver time or fetches through a
+separate clock. Missing witness/auth, invalid time and the bounded timeout
+refuse explicitly. This is clock observation, not proof of business settlement.
+
+loadFixtures validates options.url before URL parsing and names the rejected
+persona or SHA-256 credential fingerprint without printing bearer bytes.
+startStatic names build:web or build from the project package scripts when
+its output directory is absent. withBrowser resolves playwright-core first,
+with an actionable missing-peer refusal before creating logs or launching a
+browser; the full day-two recipe enters withBrowser before build/deploy.
+
+The source-generated proof API table includes every runtime and type export,
+signatures, result fields and @throws reasons. The packed qualification executes
+the literal wire blocks and production-account day-two script unchanged, plus
+a 30-day two-schedule coalesced advance, explicit cap refusal, memory-host replay
+and array alias case. The production starter's web select labels use explicit
+htmlFor/id pairs. Production entry/auth UI behavior remains with its owning
+production workstream; this amendment changes those label associations only.

new file mode 100644

+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/proof_api.md
@@ -0,0 +1,42 @@
+## Proof API (generated from source)
+
+Every runtime and type export of `snapback4/proof` is listed below. Signatures, result fields and failure reasons are generated from TypeScript declarations and their @throws comments; qualification checks this table against the source. Playwright types such as Page and Locator come from playwright-core.
+
+| Signature | Result / fields | Failure reasons |
+|---|---|---|
+| `bounded<T>(work: Promise<T>, ms: number, label: string): Promise<T>` | `Awaited<T>` | deadline expires; work rejection propagates. |
+| `cleanup(...steps: Array<(() => unknown &#124; Promise<unknown>) &#124; undefined>): Promise<void>` | `void` | AggregateError after every cleanup was attempted; each step has a 10-second bound. |
+| `cli(args: readonly string[], options?: ProofOptions): Promise<CliResult>` | `{ code: number &#124; null; signal: NodeJS.Signals &#124; null; stdout: string; stderr: string; logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string; } }` | spawn error or command deadline; nonzero exit is returned in code, not thrown. |
+| `command(binary: string, args: readonly string[], options?: ProofOptions): Promise<CliResult>` | `{ code: number &#124; null; signal: NodeJS.Signals &#124; null; stdout: string; stderr: string; logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string; } }` | spawn error or command deadline; nonzero exit is returned in code, not thrown. |
+| `deploy(dir: string, schema: string, options?: ProofOptions): Promise<CliResult>` | `{ code: number &#124; null; signal: NodeJS.Signals &#124; null; stdout: string; stderr: string; logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string; } }` | missing explicit saved source, CLI failure, spawn error or deadline. |
+| `fillRN(locator: Locator, text: string): Promise<void>` | `void` | locator ambiguity, disabled/closed control, or Playwright deadline. |
+| `frontPage(page: Page): Promise<Page>` | `Page` | page closed or browser activation failed. |
+| `loadFixtures(directory: string, options: LoadFixturesOptions): Promise<FixtureRow[]>` | `Array of { table: string; row: Record<string, unknown> }` | E_PROOF_USAGE for missing url or invalid timeout; E_AUTH naming attempted credential; catalog, clock, filesystem, row JSON/table or E_FIXTURE_TIME validation failure. |
+| `logsDir(options?: Pick<ProofOptions, "cwd" &#124; "logsDir">): string` | `string` | None; returns the resolved explicit or cwd/logs path. |
+| `portRange(env?: NodeJS.ProcessEnv): string` | `string` | partial, invalid or reversed PORT_LO/PORT_HI pair. |
+| `renderedText(page: Page, text: string &#124; RegExp, options?: { within?: Locator; exact?: boolean; }): Locator` | `Locator` | None at construction; locator observation can refuse ambiguous or invalid selectors. |
+| `rollback(dir: string, to: number &#124; "latest", options?: ProofOptions): Promise<CliResult>` | `{ code: number &#124; null; signal: NodeJS.Signals &#124; null; stdout: string; stderr: string; logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string; } }` | invalid explicit release target, CLI failure, spawn error or deadline. |
+| `sandboxUnavailable(error: unknown): boolean` | `false or true` | None; returns a boolean for recognized sandbox launch errors. |
+| `selectByValue(locator: Locator, value: string): Promise<void>` | `void` | locator ambiguity, unavailable option or Playwright deadline. |
+| `startDev(options?: ProofOptions &#124; readonly string[]): Promise<DevHost>` | `{ personas: string[]; now: () => Promise<number>; url: string; pid: number; logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string; }; stop: () => Promise<void> }` | invalid port range or conflicting memory flags; child exits, readiness deadline, PID/served identity mismatch, or unavailable dev clock. |
+| `startServe(deployDir: string, options?: ProofOptions &#124; readonly string[]): Promise<ProofHost>` | `{ url: string; pid: number; logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string; }; stop: () => Promise<void> }` | E_PROOF_USAGE for missing deployment directory; child exit, readiness deadline or served identity mismatch. |
+| `startStatic(dir: string, options?: ProofOptions): Promise<ProofHost>` | `{ url: string; pid: number; logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string; }; stop: () => Promise<void> }` | missing export/index or build script precondition; exhausted ports, readiness deadline or served identity mismatch. |
+| `startVite(dir: string, options?: ProofOptions): Promise<ProofHost>` | `{ url: string; pid: number; logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string; }; stop: () => Promise<void> }` | missing Vite dependency/configuration; exhausted ports, readiness deadline or served identity mismatch. |
+| `statusText(page: Page, text?: string &#124; RegExp): Locator` | `Locator` | None at construction; ambiguous text is refused when observed. |
+| `stopChild(child: ChildProcess &#124; Pick<ProofHost, "url" &#124; "stop">, graceMs?: number): Promise<void>` | `void` | invalid handle/deadline, host stop failure or TERM/KILL reap deadline. |
+| `testSource(source: string, options?: TestSourceOptions): Promise<TestSourceResult>` | `{ casesRun: number; casesSkipped: number; code: number &#124; null; signal: NodeJS.Signals &#124; null; stdout: string; stderr: string; logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string; } }` | missing source, CLI failure, invalid business-case summary or zero cases without fixtureOnly. |
+| `waitForClock(page: Page, advancedNow: number, options?: { timeoutMs?: number; }): Promise<number>` | `number` | E_PROOF_USAGE for invalid advanced time; missing/disabled witness, signed-out client, page error or page-clock deadline. |
+| `waitForFresh(locator: Locator, previous: string &#124; null, options?: number &#124; { within?: Locator; timeoutMs?: number; }): Promise<string>` | `string` | observation deadline with scoped locator, last text and visible headings; selector ambiguity is propagated. |
+| `waitForServer<T>(read: () => Promise<T>, accepts: (value: T) => boolean, options?: { timeoutMs?: number; label?: string; }): Promise<T>` | `Awaited<T>` | deadline naming label and last value; read or predicate errors propagate. |
+| `withBrowser<T>(options: LaunchOptions & Pick<ProofOptions, "cwd" &#124; "logsDir"> & { chromium?: BrowserType; }, proof: (context: BrowserContext) => Promise<T>): Promise<T>` | `Awaited<T>` | E_PROOF_BROWSER for missing playwright-core; invalid deadline, browser launch failure or callback error; bounded context/browser cleanup failure. |
+
+| Exported type | Fields |
+|---|---|
+| `CliResult` | `{ code: number &#124; null; signal: NodeJS.Signals &#124; null; stdout: string; stderr: string; logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string; } }` |
+| `DevHost` | `{ personas: string[]; now: () => Promise<number>; url: string; pid: number; logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string; }; stop: () => Promise<void> }` |
+| `FixtureRow` | `{ table: string; row: Record<string, unknown> }` |
+| `LoadFixturesOptions` | `url: string; cwd?: string; persona?: string; headers?: Record<string, string>; now?: number; timeoutMs?: number` |
+| `ProofHost` | `{ url: string; pid: number; logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string; }; stop: () => Promise<void> }` |
+| `ProofOptions` | `cwd?: string; env?: NodeJS.ProcessEnv; args?: readonly string[]; timeoutMs?: number; stopTimeoutMs?: number; logsDir?: string; secret?: boolean; detached?: boolean; livenessPipe?: boolean` |
+| `TestSourceOptions` | `fixtureOnly?: boolean; cwd?: string; env?: NodeJS.ProcessEnv; args?: readonly string[]; timeoutMs?: number; stopTimeoutMs?: number; logsDir?: string; secret?: boolean; detached?: boolean; livenessPipe?: boolean` |
+| `TestSourceResult` | `{ casesRun: number; casesSkipped: number; code: number &#124; null; signal: NodeJS.Signals &#124; null; stdout: string; stderr: string; logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string; } }` |

 not fail the run. Other refused sweep queries fail unless that query/persona's
@@ -241,7 +246,7 @@ A synchronous form onSubmit caused by that button also qualifies.

 Require state === "sent" before asserting the rendered business result.

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/testing_day_two.md b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/testing_day_two.md
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..8512f08385
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/testing_day_two.md
@@ -0,0 +1,97 @@
+
+## Day-two proof with production accounts
+
+Run this in a fresh `init --web` starter after installing its dependencies.
+Save the block as scripts/day2-accounts.mjs and run `node scripts/day2-accounts.mjs`.
+It uses the starter's complete production recipe (teams and memberships),
+compiles and builds that UI, then signs in through its production account form.
+The production entry recipe is maintained on `guide production`.
+Adapt the assertions to your own business model when replacing this example.
+
+The proof keeps one live serve process and one browser page through v1 → v2 →
+rollback → forward. It tests real password accounts, a rendered durable write,
+existing-row defaults, a new membership, authorization refusals, and retained
+rows at every transition. Saved sources and deployments belong to its private
+scratch directory. It changes the fresh starter's working schema and UI to the
+production recipe; run it in a copy when preserving an existing application.
+
+<!-- DAY TWO ACCOUNTS BEGIN -->
+```js
+import assert from "node:assert/strict";
+import { copyFile, mkdir, mkdtemp, readFile, writeFile, rm } from "node:fs/promises";
+import { join } from "node:path";
+import { signup } from "snapback4/auth";
+import { withBrowser, command, cli, deploy, rollback, startServe, startStatic,
+  fillRN, bounded, renderedText, waitForFresh, waitForServer, cleanup } from "snapback4/proof";
+
+await withBrowser({ executablePath: process.env.CHROME }, async context => {
+  // Browser dependency checks happen before building or deploying anything.
+  for (const [from, to] of [["schema.q", "snapback/schema.q"], ["App.tsx", "src/App.tsx"], ["client.ts", "src/client.ts"]]) {
+    await copyFile(`recipes/production/${from}`, to);
+  }
+  for (const args of [["check"]]) { const result = await cli(args); assert.equal(result.code, 0, result.stderr); }
+  for (const script of ["typecheck", "build"]) {
+    const result = await command("npm", ["run", script]); assert.equal(result.code, 0, result.stderr);
+  }
+  const work = await mkdtemp(join(process.cwd(), ".day2-accounts-"));
+  let serve, web;
+  try {
+    const v1 = join(work, "v1"), v2 = join(work, "v2"), directory = join(work, "deployment");
+    await mkdir(v1); await mkdir(v2);
+    const base = await readFile("recipes/production/schema.q", "utf8");
+    const evolved = base.replace("table members:\n", "table members:\n  note: text <=40 default 'evolved'\n");
+    assert.notEqual(evolved, base);
+    await writeFile(join(v1, "schema.q"), base); await writeFile(join(v2, "schema.q"), evolved);
+    await deploy(directory, v1);
+    serve = await startServe(directory); web = await startStatic("dist");
+    const password = "Proof-password-42!", email = `admin-${crypto.randomUUID()}@example.test`;
+    const admin = await signup(serve.url, email, password);
+    const member = await signup(serve.url, `member-${crypto.randomUUID()}@example.test`, password);
+    const outsider = await signup(serve.url, `outsider-${crypto.randomUUID()}@example.test`, password);
+    for (const account of [admin, member, outsider]) assert.equal(account.ok, true, JSON.stringify(account));
+    const page = await context.newPage();
+    await page.goto(`${web.url}/?api=${encodeURIComponent(serve.url)}`, { waitUntil: "domcontentloaded" });
+    await fillRN(page.getByLabel("Email", { exact: true }), email);
+    await fillRN(page.getByLabel("Password", { exact: true }), password);
+    await page.getByRole("button", { name: "Sign in", exact: true }).click();
+    await waitForFresh(renderedText(page, "Teams and roles"), null);
+    assert.equal(await page.getByRole("button", { name: /^Dev:/ }).count(), 0, "serve offers accounts only");
+    const title = `Team ${crypto.randomUUID()}`;
+    await fillRN(page.getByLabel("Team name", { exact: true }), title);
+    const create = page.getByRole("button", { name: "Create team", exact: true });
+    await create.evaluate((button, title) => window.__snapback4Witness.beginForm("createTeam", { title }, button), title);
+    await create.click();
+    const receipt = await bounded(page.evaluate(() => window.__snapback4Witness.formResult()), 30000, "team receipt");
+    assert.equal(receipt.state, "sent", JSON.stringify(receipt));
+    const teamId = receipt.result.id;
+    async function call(account, door, name, args) {
+      const response = await fetch(`${serve.url}/${door}/${name}`, { method: "POST",
+        headers: { "content-type": "application/json", authorization: `Bearer ${account.session.token}` },
+        body: JSON.stringify({ ...(door === "m" ? { id: crypto.randomUUID() } : {}), args }) });
+      assert.equal(response.status, 200); return response.json();
+    }
+    async function business(label, evolved, joined) {
+      const own = await waitForServer(() => call(admin, "q", "myRole", { teamId }), read => read.complete === true, { label });
+      assert.equal(own.data.principal, admin.session.principal); assert.equal(own.data.role, "admin");
+      assert.equal(own.data.note, evolved ? "evolved" : undefined, label);
+      const roster = await call(admin, "q", "roster", { teamId, c: null });
+      assert.equal(roster.complete, true); assert.equal(roster.data.length, joined ? 2 : 1, label);
+      assert.ok(roster.data.some(row => row.principal === admin.session.principal));
+      if (joined) assert.ok(roster.data.some(row => row.principal === member.session.principal));
+      assert.equal((await call(outsider, "q", "myRole", { teamId })).denied.code, "NOT_MEMBER", label);
+      const refused = await call(outsider, "m", "invite", { teamId, principal: outsider.session.principal, role: "admin" });
+      assert.equal(refused.state, "failed"); assert.equal(refused.why.code, "NOT_ADMIN", label);
+      await waitForFresh(renderedText(page, new RegExp(teamId), { within: page.locator("main") }), null);
+    }
+    await business("v1", false, false);
+    await deploy(directory, v2);
+    const invited = await call(admin, "m", "invite", { teamId, principal: member.session.principal, role: "member" });
+    assert.equal(invited.state, "sent", JSON.stringify(invited));
+    await business("v2", true, true);
+    await rollback(directory, 1); await business("rollback-v1", false, true);
+    await rollback(directory, "latest"); await business("forward-v2", true, true);
+    console.log("day-two accounts passed: v1, hot v2, rollback, forward; business assertions at every step");
+  } finally { await cleanup(() => web?.stop(), () => serve?.stop(), () => rm(work, { recursive: true, force: true })); }
+});
+```
+<!-- DAY TWO ACCOUNTS END -->
diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/wire.md b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/wire.md
index 42262d3c90..04976ab2a1 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/wire.md
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/wire.md
@@ -2,16 +2,100 @@
 
 Every call carries a principal; public visitors use guest sessions; `serve` accepts bearers only. Plain JSON over HTTP works with curl, scripts and non-JS clients.
 
-```sh
-U=http://127.0.0.1:4400
-curl -s -X POST $U/q/mine -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice' -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"args":{}}'
-curl -s -X POST $U/m/add -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice' -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"args":{"body":"hi"}}'
+## First raw HTTP proof
+
+| Send | Inspect the JSON body |
+|---|---|
+| `POST /q/name {"args":{…}}` | `{data, complete}` or `{denied:{…}}` |
+| `POST /m/name {"id":"one-intent","args":{…}}` | `{state:"sent", result}` or `{state:"failed", why}` |
+| `POST /seed [{"table":"notes","row":{…}}]` | Bare array; `{seeded}` or `{denied}` |
+| `POST /dev/reset {"seed":[{"table":"notes","row":{…}}]}` | Wrapper; `{reset:true, seeded, …}` or `{denied}` |
+
+Dev uses `x-snapback-persona: alice`. Serve uses `Authorization: Bearer <token>`
+from a real account or service seat. Send one credential with `Content-Type:
+application/json`. Authentication can refuse HTTP 401 before either operation;
+HTTP 200 still requires inspecting `denied`, `state`, and `why`.
+Relative times such as `"now+1h"` work in typed seed/fixture time fields and
+fixture case arguments. `/m` takes Unix milliseconds; it never interprets
+relative time strings. Query data and mutation results have different envelopes.
+
+Save this schema as snapback/schema.q in a scratch project with snapback4
+installed. Save the JavaScript as scripts/wire-proof.mjs and run it with Node.
+Qualification executes these exact blocks through the packed CLI and generates
+the trace below. Minted identifiers, timestamps and observed durations alone
+are normalized; refusal codes, request bodies and envelope fields are retained.
+
+<!-- WIRE FIRST SCHEMA BEGIN -->
+```quarry
+use personas alice, bob
+table notes:
+  title: text <=80
+  at: time
+  public 'literal wire proof'
+  insert <- allow
+  online only
+query list(c: cursor ?):
+  require viewer = 'dev:alice' else READ_REFUSED
+  return notes first 10 by .id after c
+mutation add(title: text <=80, at: time):
+  require title != 'refuse' else WRITE_REFUSED
+  return insert notes { title, at }
 ```
+<!-- WIRE FIRST SCHEMA END -->
+
+<!-- WIRE FIRST SCRIPT BEGIN -->
+```js
+import assert from "node:assert/strict";
+import { startDev, cleanup } from "snapback4/proof";
+const dev = await startDev();
+const trace = [];
+async function post(path, body, persona = "alice") {
+  const headers = { "content-type": "application/json", "x-snapback-persona": persona };
+  const response = await fetch(dev.url + path, { method: "POST", headers, body: JSON.stringify(body) });
+  const result = await response.json();
+  trace.push({ request: { method: "POST", path, headers, body }, response: { status: response.status, body: result } });
+  return result;
+}
+try {
+  const seed = [{ table: "notes", row: { id: "seed", title: "Seed", at: "now+1h" } }];
+  assert.equal((await post("/seed", seed)).seeded, 1);
+  const reset = await post("/dev/reset", { seed });
+  assert.equal(reset.reset, true); assert.notEqual(reset.storeId, reset.previousStoreId);
+  const at = await dev.now();
+  const sent = await post("/m/add", { id: "proof-write", args: { title: "Proof", at } });
+  assert.equal(sent.state, "sent"); assert.equal(sent.id, "proof-write"); assert.ok(sent.result.id);
+  const failed = await post("/m/add", { id: "refused-write", args: { title: "refuse", at } });
+  assert.equal(failed.state, "failed"); assert.equal(failed.why.code, "WRITE_REFUSED");
+  const read = await post("/q/list", { args: { c: null } });
+  assert.equal(read.complete, true); assert.equal(read.data.length, 2);
+  assert.equal((await post("/q/list", { args: { c: null } }, "bob")).denied.code, "READ_REFUSED");
+  assert.equal((await post("/m/add", { args: { title: "Relative", at: "now+1h" } })).why.code, "E_INPUT");
+  const normalize = (value, key = "") => {
+    if (Array.isArray(value)) return value.map(item => normalize(item));
+    if (value && typeof value === "object") return Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(value).map(([k,v]) => [k, normalize(v,k)]));
+    if (["store_id", "storeId", "previousStoreId"].includes(key)) return "<store-id>";
+    if (key === "id" && !["seed", "proof-write", "refused-write"].includes(value)) return "<row-id>";
+    if (["ms", "skewMs"].includes(key)) return "<observed-ms>";
+    return typeof value === "number" && value > 1_000_000_000_000 ? "<unix-ms>" : value;
+  };
+  console.log(JSON.stringify(normalize(trace), null, 2));
+} finally { await cleanup(() => dev.stop()); }
+```
+<!-- WIRE FIRST SCRIPT END -->
 
-`snapback4 dev` serves on that address (choose another with `--port N`, or
-`--port 0` for a free one; the first stdout line is the URL). A taken port
-refuses the start at once with `E_ADDRINUSE`, naming the holder's pid. The JS client (`snapback4/client`) stays the
-paved surface for apps: it adds sync, offline writes and live queries.
+<!-- WIRE FIRST TRACE BEGIN -->
+```json
+[
+{"request":{"method":"POST","path":"/seed","headers":{"content-type":"application/json","x-snapback-persona":"alice"},"body":[{"table":"notes","row":{"id":"seed","title":"Seed","at":"now+1h"}}]},"response":{"status":200,"body":{"seeded":1,"generation":1}}},
+{"request":{"method":"POST","path":"/dev/reset","headers":{"content-type":"application/json","x-snapback-persona":"alice"},"body":{"seed":[{"table":"notes","row":{"id":"seed","title":"Seed","at":"now+1h"}}]}},"response":{"status":200,"body":{"assetsRemoved":0,"clockOffsetMs":0,"generation":1,"head":3,"ms":"<observed-ms>","now":"<unix-ms>","previousStoreId":"<store-id>","reset":true,"seeded":1,"skewMs":"<observed-ms>","storeId":"<store-id>"}}},
+{"request":{"method":"POST","path":"/m/add","headers":{"content-type":"application/json","x-snapback-persona":"alice"},"body":{"id":"proof-write","args":{"title":"Proof","at":"<unix-ms>"}}},"response":{"status":200,"body":{"generation":1,"head":4,"id":"proof-write","preserved":["notes"],"result":{"at":"<unix-ms>","id":"<row-id>","title":"Proof"},"seq":4,"state":"sent","store_id":"<store-id>"}}},
+{"request":{"method":"POST","path":"/m/add","headers":{"content-type":"application/json","x-snapback-persona":"alice"},"body":{"id":"refused-write","args":{"title":"refuse","at":"<unix-ms>"}}},"response":{"status":200,"body":{"generation":1,"head":4,"id":"refused-write","preserved":[],"state":"failed","store_id":"<store-id>","why":{"code":"WRITE_REFUSED","family":"rule","guide":"programs","message":"the program refused with WRITE_REFUSED","rule":"WRITE_REFUSED","site":"schema.q:12:3"}}}},
+{"request":{"method":"POST","path":"/q/list","headers":{"content-type":"application/json","x-snapback-persona":"alice"},"body":{"args":{"c":null}}},"response":{"status":200,"body":{"scopes":{},"generation":1,"complete":true,"cut":null,"data":[{"at":"<unix-ms>","id":"<row-id>","title":"Proof"},{"at":"<unix-ms>","id":"seed","title":"Seed"}],"next":null,"nextExpiry":null,"seq":4,"store_id":"<store-id>","tables":["notes"],"viewer":"dev:alice","meter":{"cut":null,"examined":2,"heapBytes":635,"indexEntries":0,"outputBytes":198,"probes":0,"rowsWritten":0,"steps":7}}}},
+{"request":{"method":"POST","path":"/q/list","headers":{"content-type":"application/json","x-snapback-persona":"bob"},"body":{"args":{"c":null}}},"response":{"status":200,"body":{"complete":false,"denied":{"code":"READ_REFUSED","family":"rule","guide":"programs","message":"the program refused with READ_REFUSED","rule":"READ_REFUSED","site":"schema.q:9:3"},"generation":1,"head":4,"seq":4,"store_id":"<store-id>"}}},
+{"request":{"method":"POST","path":"/m/add","headers":{"content-type":"application/json","x-snapback-persona":"alice"},"body":{"args":{"title":"Relative","at":"now+1h"}}},"response":{"status":200,"body":{"generation":1,"head":4,"state":"failed","store_id":"<store-id>","why":{"code":"E_INPUT","family":"input","guide":"types","message":"argument at: expected a time in milliseconds, got a string"}}}}
+]
+```
+<!-- WIRE FIRST TRACE END -->
 
 ## Identity
 
@@ -186,7 +270,7 @@ Dev only, same machine; `serve` refuses each one.
   (signed-up users, guests). The adopted schema, personas and service seats
   (`snapback4 token`, the dev worker's) stay. The store takes a new identity:
   a local client re-acquires the new store by itself, an online client halts
-  with `E_STORE_REPLACED` (`createApp` reopens it). The head keeps counting.
+  with `E_DEV_RESET: reopen the client` (`createApp` reopens it). The head keeps counting.
   The body is empty, a `/seed` array, or `{"seed": [..], "clock": "reset"}`
   (either key optional): seed rows are written as `/seed` writes them, in the
   same transaction (a refused row resets nothing), and `"clock": "reset"`

+For browser proofs, `guide testing --section 5` documents the web witness.
+Use `beginForm(name, null, button)` when dynamic arguments should be claimed
+without exact argument matching; require its sent receipt before asserting UI.

 own Release build, or run an already installed app, then retain receipts and video.

-    assert_eq!(skipped.len(), 999);
-    assert_eq!(skipped[0]["command"], "snapback4 run tick '{}'");
+    assert_eq!(skipped.len(), 32);
+    assert_eq!(reply["schedules"][0]["skippedBoundariesOmitted"], 967);
+    let large = f.command(&["clock", "advance", "4000m", "--each-boundary", "--port", port]);
+    assert!(large.stdout.len() > 65_536, "explicit windows exercise the former transport ceiling");
+    assert_eq!(output_json(large)["schedules"][0]["firedWindows"].as_array().unwrap().len(), 2000);
+    assert_eq!(skipped[0]["command"], format!("snapback4 run tick '{{}}' --port {port}"));

@@ -1204,3 +1208,48 @@ fn kit3_clock_reply_and_preflight_use_the_persisted_authoritative_floor() {
     assert!((actual - advanced["now"].as_i64().unwrap()).abs() < 1000, "{advanced}; actual={actual}");
     assert!(advanced["skewMs"].as_i64().unwrap() > 7_000_000, "{advanced}");
 }
+
+#[test]
+fn kit4_forty_day_coalesced_advance_has_no_window_cap() {
+    let f = Fixture::new("kit4-long-jump");
+    fs::write(f.0.join("snapback/schema.q"), SOURCE.replace("every 2m:", "every 5m:")).unwrap();
+    let host = f.start(&["dev", "--memory", "--no-watch"]);
+    host.post("/m/counter", json!({"args":{}}));
+    let refused = host.post("/dev/clock/advance", json!({"duration":"40d", "eachBoundary":true}));
+    assert!(refused["denied"]["message"].as_str().unwrap().contains("omit --each-boundary"), "{refused}");
+    assert_eq!(host.request("GET", "/dev/status", json!({}))["clockOffsetMs"], 0);
+    let reply = host.post("/dev/clock/advance", json!({"duration":"40d"}));
+    assert!(reply.get("denied").is_none(), "{reply}");
+    let schedule = &reply["schedules"][0];
+    assert_eq!(schedule["firedWindows"].as_array().unwrap().len(), 1);
+    let skipped = schedule["skippedPeriods"].as_i64().unwrap();
+    assert!((11519..=11520).contains(&skipped), "{schedule}");
+    assert_eq!(schedule["skippedBoundaries"].as_array().unwrap().len(), 32);
+    assert_eq!(schedule["skippedBoundariesOmitted"], skipped - 32);
+    assert_eq!(host.query("counters")[0]["n"], 1);
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn kit4_clock_offsets_are_exact_and_refusals_leave_them_unchanged() {
+    let f=Fixture::new("kit4-offset");
+    let host=f.start(&["dev","--memory","--no-watch"]);
+    let status=||host.request("GET","/dev/status",json!({}));
+    assert_eq!(status()["clockOffsetMs"],0);
+    host.post("/dev/clock/advance",json!({"duration":"-1ms"}));
+    assert_eq!(status()["clockOffsetMs"],0);
+    for n in 1..=2 {
+        let reply=host.post("/dev/clock/advance",json!({"duration":"3h","eachBoundary":true}));
+        assert_eq!(reply["offsetMs"],n*10_800_000);
+        let schedule=&reply["schedules"][0];
+        for window in schedule["firedWindows"].as_array().unwrap() {
+            assert!(schedule["reason"].as_str().unwrap().contains(&utc_test(window["periodEnd"].as_i64().unwrap())),"{schedule}");
+        }
+        assert_eq!(status()["clockOffsetMs"],n*10_800_000);
+    }
+    assert_eq!(host.post("/dev/reset",json!({"clock":"reset"}))["clockOffsetMs"],0);
+}
+fn utc_test(ms:i64)->String {
+    // The report's UTC minute is enough to distinguish each fired window.
+    let minute=(ms/60_000)%60; let hour=(ms/3_600_000)%24;
+    format!("{hour:02}:{minute:02}:00Z")
+}

 and all earlier received rounds are retained with provenance. Later commits

 in the test harness, and guide/census assertions and custom-Home qualification scope; they add no semantic contract.
+
+## 12. Wave 15 proof diagnostics and clock sampling (KIT4)
+
+Implementer: Codex, 2026-10-18, commissioned for 0.4.32. On a canonical
+argument mismatch the shared E_WITNESS_UNCLAIMED message names the differing
+top-level keys. withBrowser installs a diagnostic binding which writes the
+full armed and actual arguments to a fresh mode-0600 *.witness.private.jsonl
+file under the proof log directory. Receipt messages contain no argument values.
+Diagnostic failure never cancels, retries or blocks the application's write.
+This supersedes §11's suppression of argument values in all retained logs;
+private proof logs intentionally contain sensitive application evidence.
+
+Substring timeout advice requires an explicitly scoped renderedText or
+waitForFresh locator and a substring within that scope. Whole-page prose
+cannot trigger the hint. A timeout includes up to three visible page headings
+(300 characters total); it does not weaken exact matching or ambiguity errors.
+
+waitForClock(page, advancedNow, {timeoutMs=30000}) polls the registered
+witness's signed-in client.now(), returning its number once it reaches the
+advance reply's now. It never sets page/driver time or fetches through a
+separate clock. Missing witness/auth, invalid time and the bounded timeout
+refuse explicitly. This is clock observation, not proof of business settlement.
+
+loadFixtures validates options.url before URL parsing and names the rejected
+persona or SHA-256 credential fingerprint without printing bearer bytes.
+startStatic names build:web or build from the project package scripts when
+its output directory is absent. withBrowser resolves playwright-core first,
+with an actionable missing-peer refusal before creating logs or launching a
+browser; the full day-two recipe enters withBrowser before build/deploy.
+
+The source-generated proof API table includes every runtime and type export,
+signatures, result fields and @throws reasons. The packed qualification executes
+the literal wire blocks and production-account day-two script unchanged, plus
+a 30-day two-schedule coalesced advance, explicit cap refusal, memory-host replay
+and array alias case. The production starter's web select labels use explicit
+htmlFor/id pairs. Production entry/auth UI behavior remains with its owning
+production workstream; this amendment changes those label associations only.

### 20.12.1 Wave 14 development clock windows (KIT3, 2026-10-16)

Implementer: Codex, 2026-10-16. The ordinary scheduler and default clock advance
keep latest-window coalescing: production must not replay an unbounded backlog
of effects after downtime. Development tests can request `clock advance
--each-boundary` (HTTP `eachBoundary:true`) to admit every missed window in
chronological boundary order, with declaration order breaking ties. Jobs drain
in that order on the final advanced clock; their periodStart/periodEnd arguments
name historical windows, while `now` remains the final advanced time. This is backlog admission, not
a historical simulation: test period-dependent work using periodStart/periodEnd,
not now. To test job logic dependent on now at each boundary, issue separate
advances. Scheduled
actions are admitted in order at the final advanced time (their deadlines
begin at admission, never at a historical periodEnd); external workers retain
their normal async execution contract. First-seen/changed schedules only prime their current
boundary. Held schedules remain held. Boundary/job admission keeps the existing
atomic saved-boundary transaction; restart never replays an admitted boundary.

Preflight always validates report metadata, including with held/replay schedules;
the hold only suppresses boundary enumeration/admission. Under --each-boundary it enumerates at most 10,000 crossed windows in total across all schedules, before
moving time or leases. A conservative report-size estimate also refuses above
15 MiB, reserving 1 MiB for the outer clock/work envelope inside the host's
16 MiB JSON encoder (and the existing 64 MiB CLI HTTP exchange).
Retained job summaries project only bounded fields; error previews are capped
at 1024 UTF-8 bytes plus an ellipsis, with errorTruncated and the run id. The
preflight includes the worst JSON escaping cost for that preview per schedule;
full error/args/cursor payloads are never loaded into the report.
Larger requests refuse E_CLOCK_INPUT and advise a smaller
advance; this bounds both execution and the explicit report. Each schedule's
reply includes firedWindows and skippedBoundaries (periodStart, periodEnd and,
for each skipped job window, its literal `snapback4 run <job>` command).
Coalesced advances have no crossed-window cap: they execute only the latest window
per schedule, retain the exact skippedPeriods count, and preview the first 32
skippedBoundaries per schedule with skippedBoundariesOmitted naming the remainder.
Report preflight budgets that bounded preview independently of jump distance.
Calendar schedules use calendar previous/next, never fixed-duration division.
An incomplete drain reports the already advanced now/skewMs and a refusal;
shutdown specifically refuses non-retryable E_CLOCK_INTERRUPTED rather than
turning a stopped job/delivery loop into successful HTTP 200;
callers inspect it and never blindly retry an advance. Ordinary maintenance
continues queued work; `run <job>` can inspect a run and `reset --clock reset`
provides an explicit fresh-test recovery if the scenario should be restarted. The default retains
existing fired/periodStart/periodEnd fields and adds the explicit windows.

Successful dev reset, clock status and clock advance replies include `now` and
`skewMs` (served time minus sampled wall time). The existing durable timed-
disclosure floor participates in both reported now and window preflight; they
cannot describe an earlier raw process-clock value after a persisted restart.
This reports the existing authoritative floor without changing its lifetime law.
Existing offset fields remain.
Reset keeps its current time unless clock:reset was requested. Error replies
after a clock change include the same clock evidence. No serve time controls,
clock rewind, job authority or production scheduler change is introduced.


Implementation review record (2026-10-17): the final KIT3 amendments and semantic
implementation at `c94694f263` received independent **READY** verdicts from
[Muse Spark 1.3](./reviews/3000.011.003.000.000-wave14-kit3-r8.muse.md) and a
[fresh Codex gpt-6.1-sol session](./reviews/3000.011.003.000.000-wave14-kit3-r8.codex.md).
The [identical blind brief](./reviews/3000.011.003.000.000-wave14-kit3-r8.brief.md)
and all earlier received rounds are retained with provenance. Later commits
`e0af692817` and `74ee49d4df` correct the Expo thread's proof scope, configured browser selection
in the test harness, and guide/census assertions and custom-Home qualification scope; they add no semantic contract.

+#[test]
+fn kit4_forty_day_coalesced_advance_has_no_window_cap() {
+    let f = Fixture::new("kit4-long-jump");

+    assert!(refused["denied"]["message"].as_str().unwrap().contains("omit --each-boundary"), "{refused}");

+    let skipped = schedule["skippedPeriods"].as_i64().unwrap();
+    assert!((11519..=11520).contains(&skipped), "{schedule}");
+    assert_eq!(schedule["skippedBoundaries"].as_array().unwrap().len(), 32);
+    assert_eq!(schedule["skippedBoundariesOmitted"], skipped - 32);
+    assert_eq!(host.query("counters")[0]["n"], 1);

+
+#[test]
+fn kit4_clock_offsets_are_exact_and_refusals_leave_them_unchanged() {
+    let f=Fixture::new("kit4-offset");
+    let host=f.start(&["dev","--memory","--no-watch"]);
+    let status=||host.request("GET","/dev/status",json!({}));
+    assert_eq!(status()["clockOffsetMs"],0);
+    host.post("/dev/clock/advance",json!({"duration":"-1ms"}));
+    assert_eq!(status()["clockOffsetMs"],0);
+    for n in 1..=2 {
+        let reply=host.post("/dev/clock/advance",json!({"duration":"3h","eachBoundary":true}));
+        assert_eq!(reply["offsetMs"],n*10_800_000);
+        let schedule=&reply["schedules"][0];
+        for window in schedule["firedWindows"].as_array().unwrap() {
+            assert!(schedule["reason"].as_str().unwrap().contains(&utc_test(window["periodEnd"].as_i64().unwrap())),"{schedule}");
+        }
+        assert_eq!(status()["clockOffsetMs"],n*10_800_000);
+    }
+    assert_eq!(host.post("/dev/reset",json!({"clock":"reset"}))["clockOffsetMs"],0);
+}
+fn utc_test(ms:i64)->String {
+    // The report's UTC minute is enough to distinguish each fired window.
+    let minute=(ms/60_000)%60; let hour=(ms/3_600_000)%24;
+    format!("{hour:02}:{minute:02}:00Z")
+}

+test('KIT4 missing URL refuses before URL parsing', async () => {
+  for (const options of [undefined, {}, {url:''}, {url:42}]) await assert.rejects(loadFixtures('.', options), /loadFixtures requires options.url/);
+});
+test('KIT4 fixture auth errors identify persona or token fingerprint without leaking bearer bytes', async t => {
+  const server=createServer((req,res)=>{res.writeHead(401,{'content-type':'application/json'});res.end('{"denied":{"code":"E_AUTH"}}');});
+  await new Promise(resolve=>server.listen(0,'127.0.0.1',resolve));
+  t.after(()=>new Promise(resolve=>server.close(resolve)));
+  const url=`http://127.0.0.1:${server.address().port}`;
+  await assert.rejects(loadFixtures('.',{url,persona:'mallory'}),/using persona "mallory"/);
+  await assert.rejects(loadFixtures('.',{url,headers:{authorization:'Bearer private-token'}}),error=>{
+    assert.match(error.message,/using token sha256:[0-9a-f]{12}/);assert.doesNotMatch(error.message,/private-token/);return true;
+  });
 });

diff --git a/snapback4/packages/snapback4/test/wire-first.test.mjs b/snapback4/packages/snapback4/test/wire-first.test.mjs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..bd16ec5698
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/packages/snapback4/test/wire-first.test.mjs
@@ -0,0 +1,31 @@
+import test from 'node:test';
+import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
+import {mkdtempSync,mkdirSync,readFileSync,writeFileSync,symlinkSync,rmSync} from 'node:fs';
+import {join,resolve} from 'node:path';
+import {spawnSync} from 'node:child_process';
+const root=resolve(import.meta.dirname,'../../..');
+const binary=process.env.SNAPBACK4_BIN??join(root,'target/debug/snapback4');
+const installed=process.env.SNAPBACK4_PROOF_PACKAGE??join(root,'packages/snapback4');
+const source=join(root,'crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/wire.md');
+const block=(text,name,language)=>text.match(new RegExp(`<!-- WIRE FIRST ${name} BEGIN -->\\n\x60\x60\x60${language}\\n([\\s\\S]*?)\\n\x60\x60\x60\\n<!-- WIRE FIRST ${name} END -->`))?.[1];
+test('KIT4 literal first wire page runs unchanged and its trace is generated from real requests',{timeout:60000},t=>{
+  let guide;
+  if(process.env.KIT4_UPDATE_WIRE==='1') guide=readFileSync(source,'utf8');
+  else {const result=spawnSync(binary,['guide','wire','--full'],{encoding:'utf8',timeout:10000});assert.equal(result.status,0,result.stderr);guide=result.stdout;}
+  const cwd=mkdtempSync(join(root,'target/kit4-wire-'));t.after(()=>rmSync(cwd,{recursive:true,force:true}));
+  mkdirSync(join(cwd,'snapback'));mkdirSync(join(cwd,'node_modules'));
+  symlinkSync(installed,join(cwd,'node_modules/snapback4'),'dir');
+  writeFileSync(join(cwd,'package.json'),'{"type":"module"}');
+  writeFileSync(join(cwd,'snapback/schema.q'),block(guide,'SCHEMA','quarry'));
+  writeFileSync(join(cwd,'wire-proof.mjs'),block(guide,'SCRIPT','js'));
+  const result=spawnSync(process.execPath,['wire-proof.mjs'],{cwd,env:{...process.env,SNAPBACK4_BIN:binary},encoding:'utf8',timeout:45000});
+  assert.equal(result.status,0,result.stdout+result.stderr);
+  const trace=JSON.parse(result.stdout);
+  assert.equal(trace.length,7);assert.equal(trace[6].response.status,200);
+  if(process.env.KIT4_UPDATE_WIRE==='1') {
+    guide=guide.replace(/<!-- WIRE FIRST TRACE BEGIN -->[\s\S]*?<!-- WIRE FIRST TRACE END -->/,
+      '<!-- WIRE FIRST TRACE BEGIN -->\n```json\n'+trace.map(row=>JSON.stringify(row)).join(',\n').replace(/^/,'[\n')+'\n]\n```\n<!-- WIRE FIRST TRACE END -->');
+    writeFileSync(source,guide);
+  }
+  assert.deepEqual(trace,JSON.parse(block(guide,'TRACE','json')));
+});
